  - core\kpi_catalog\README.md:66: - **Supporting KPIs** (e.g. `sales.price.list.amount`, `sales.price.net.amount`) are defined in the catalog with `kpi_role: supporting`.   - core\kpi_catalog\README.md:67: - **Data requirement:** Each KPIâ€™s `technical.lineage` lists the fact/dimension columns required for its measure (e.g. `fact_sales.List Price Amount`). The **semantic model** must provide these tables and columns; they are defined by the **domain data contracts** (`core/data_contracts/domains/`). The pipeline that builds the semantic model from the contract (e.g. Fabric table_ops) ensures the model satisfies this data need. When adding or changing KPIs, ensure the corresponding domain contract includes the required columns.

  - internal\archive\framework_evolution.md:18: - **KPIs exist but are debated, not trusted** â€” definitions and targets are reinterpreted per report or team.
  - internal\archive\framework_evolution.md:19: - **Reports explain deviations but do not support decisions** â€” no clear link to actions or ownership.
  - internal\archive\framework_evolution.md:22: - **AI and automation are discussed but not structurally prepared** â€” no single semantic interface for humans and AI.
  - internal\archive\framework_evolution.md:24: The framework exists to make the path **strategy â†’ KPIs â†’ use cases â†’ semantic models â†’ actions** explicit, governed, and reusable so that analytics becomes a **reliable steering capability**, not a cost center.
  - internal\archive\framework_evolution.md:28: - **Fewer debates, faster decisions** â€” one KPI catalog and one measure system; reports and AI use the same definitions.
  - internal\archive\framework_evolution.md:29: - **Decision-oriented analytics** â€” organized by use cases and actions (what question, what action), not by "another dashboard."
  - internal\archive\framework_evolution.md:30: - **Reuse and speed** â€” new requirements extend existing KPIs and templates instead of reinventing logic.
  - internal\archive\framework_evolution.md:31: - **Action-ready** â€” insights connect to governed action codes (triggers, evidence, ownership).
  - internal\archive\framework_evolution.md:32: - **Automation- and AI-ready** â€” structured metadata and a single semantic layer enable both "AI helps build/maintain" and "AI consumes data reliably."
  - internal\archive\framework_evolution.md:43: 1. **Best-practice semantic modeling and reports** â€” 3-30-300, action codes, standardized report templates; semantic layer as single business interface.
  - internal\archive\framework_evolution.md:44: 2. **Best-practice architecture, code-driven** â€” workspace, dataset, lakehouse, and report structure that can be **set up and torn down by code** (e.g. Bicep, Fabric APIs), not only by clicking in the portal.
  - internal\archive\framework_evolution.md:45: 3. **Foundation for AI** â€” **AI as builder/maintainer:** metadata and structure enable AI to help create and maintain semantic models and reports in a governed way. **AI as consumer:** the semantic layer is the single, governed interface for querying and explaining data so AI and humans use the same definitions.
  - internal\archive\framework_evolution.md:46: 4. **Ease, efficiency, maintainability, scalability** â€” as **easy**, **time- and resource-saving**, and **maintenance-friendly** as possible; **scalable** to more use cases, workspaces, and customers.
  - internal\archive\framework_evolution.md:47: 5. **First, pragmatic governance** â€” lightweight but binding: ownership, change flow (e.g. KPI catalog â†’ use cases â†’ semantic model), and quality gates (e.g. Stage 1) without heavy process.
  - internal\archive\framework_evolution.md:57: ### Role 1 â€” Structured Procedure (Methodology)
  - internal\archive\framework_evolution.md:63: ### Role 2 â€” Standardized Reports Product
  - internal\archive\framework_evolution.md:65: Because of the structured approach, we can provide **standardized, pre-built reports** that nearly all companies need for their strategic goals â€” in an optimized, consistent form. These reports are the **next end product**: they implement the same use cases, KPIs, and 3-30-300 (or agreed variants) so that customers get best-practice content out of the box, not only the method to build it. Quality and consistency are high because every report is derived from the same framework artifacts.
  - internal\archive\framework_evolution.md:72: - **Framework + standardized reports:** Customer uses the procedure and additionally adopts pre-built reports â€” all of them, or a subset (e.g. Commercial pack only, or only Executive view). Faster time to value; consistency with the frameworkâ€™s definition of "done."
  - internal\archive\framework_evolution.md:149: - **AI as consumer:** The semantic layer is the single interface for querying and explaining data. AI can answer questions, explain deltas, and suggest actions using **only** governed measures and action codes â€” no ad-hoc logic. Use cases of "AI as consumer" extend the **coverage of domains and topics** that can be analysed conversationally, without building a new report for every question.
  - internal\archive\framework_evolution.md:152: **Future consideration (later stage): agentic BI development.** For a later stage, consider the building blocks and scenarios in [SQLBI: Introducing AI and agentic development for Business Intelligence](https://www.sqlbi.com/articles/introducing-ai-and-agentic-development-for-business-intelligence/): (1) chatbot tools with basic context, (2) augmented chatbot with MCP servers and curated context (PBIP + remote repos), (3) agentic developmentâ€”agents that read/write metadata and assist report/model build with orchestration and validation, (4) asynchronous agents (delegated tasks, PR-based review). Our setup (PBIP/TMDL, MCP tooling, governed context in KPI catalog and use cases) aligns with the articleâ€™s emphasis on context, tools, and environment; any agentic use should remain under â€œhumans approve, AI does not invent definitionsâ€ and pass Stage 1.
  - internal\archive\framework_evolution.md:156: - **Best-practice architecture per platform:** The vision includes **reference architectures** for different data platforms and visualization tools (e.g. Microsoft Fabric, other cloud analytics stacks). These are not only "how to model" but **deployable, code-driven setups** (workspaces, datasets, pipelines, lakehouse, report structure) so that analytics can be **set up in a day** â€” or at least in a predictable, repeatable way â€” instead of weeks of manual configuration. First full reference: Fabric / Power BI (Aurora); additional platforms follow as adapters to the same framework.

  - internal\archive\framework_evolution.md:441: - **Real-time or streaming analytics** â€” The framework targets batch/refresh semantic models and reports; event-driven or sub-second streaming is out of scope unless explicitly added later as an extension.
  - internal\archive\framework_evolution.md:442: - **Replacement of operational system reporting** â€” We do not aim to replicate or replace ERP, CRM, or operational tool reporting; we provide a governed, strategy-aligned layer that may consume data from those systems.
  - internal\archive\framework_evolution.md:443: - **Full data platform or ERP implementation** â€” We define contracts, semantics, and report structure; we do not build or own the underlying transactional systems or data engineering beyond what is needed for the semantic layer and pipelines.
  - internal\archive\framework_evolution.md:444: - **Generic "data lake" or "data mesh" design** â€” Data architecture is in scope only where it directly supports the semantic layer, deployment, and governance (e.g. workspace, lakehouse, pipelines for the framework). Broader data strategy is for the customer.
  - internal\archive\framework_evolution.md:445: - **Custom commercial or licensing model** â€” How the procedure or standardized reports are offered (internal use, license, SaaS) is a business decision; the vision describes *what* we build, not the commercial wrapper.
  - internal\archive\framework_evolution.md:459: - **Customer willingness to adopt the change flow** â€” Strategy â†’ KPIs â†’ use cases â†’ semantic model works only if the organization accepts ownership and the Golden Thread. We assume sponsors and domain owners engage.
  - internal\archive\framework_evolution.md:460: - **Stable core, extensible edges** â€” Core framework (KPIs, use cases, action codes) stays relatively stable; industry packs, custom layouts, and new domains extend at the edges without breaking the core.
  - internal\archive\framework_evolution.md:461: - **One semantic layer per deployment** â€” We assume one governed semantic interface per customer or deployment; we do not design for multiple competing semantic layers in the same scope.
  - internal\archive\framework_evolution.md:485: 1. **Procedure and one platform** â€” Lock the procedure (playbooks, use cases, governance, Stage 1) and one full platform reference (Fabric/Power BI). This is V1 + Phase A/B. Everything else builds on this.

  - internal\archive\framework_evolution.md:488: 4. **Platform architecture and "analytics in a day"** â€” Extend deployable setup so new environments can be provisioned in a day. Extend to a **second platform** when the first is stable.
  - internal\archive\framework_evolution.md:489: 5. **Lifecycle and flexibility** â€” Impact awareness, versioning, industry extensions, custom layout support. Keeps the framework relevant as strategies and requirements change.
  - internal\archive\framework_evolution.md:490: 6. **Automation and AI** â€” Stage 2, generators, AI-assisted maintenance, conversational consumption. Only after semantic and governance maturity.

  - internal\project_mgmt\OPERATING_MODEL.md:75: 1. **Backlog â†’ Planned:** Triage or agent sets Milestone, Area, Priority; item is scheduled.

  - internal\vision\ACTIONREADY_HOLISTIC_MANIFESTO.md:91: ### Das Problem: â€žInformation Overloadâ€œ und kognitive LÃ¤hmung
  - internal\vision\ACTIONREADY_HOLISTIC_MANIFESTO.md:93: Die meisten Dashboards scheitern nicht an fehlenden Daten, sondern an einer Ãœberdosis davon.   - internal\vision\ACTIONREADY_HOLISTIC_MANIFESTO.md:97: ### I. 3 Sekunden (Die emotionale Orientierung â€“ System 1)
  - internal\vision\ACTIONREADY_HOLISTIC_MANIFESTO.md:99: - **Ziel**: Sofortige Beantwortung der Frage: â€žBin ich sicher oder muss ich rennen?â€œ
  - internal\vision\ACTIONREADY_HOLISTIC_MANIFESTO.md:101: - **Psychologie**: In den ersten 3 Sekunden entscheidet das Gehirn Ã¼ber die Relevanz. Wir nutzen hochgradig aggregierte Visualisierungen (Gauges, groÃŸe Karten, rote/grÃ¼ne Ampeln).
  - internal\vision\ACTIONREADY_HOLISTIC_MANIFESTO.md:103: - **World-Class Merkmal**: Wenn ein Nutzer lÃ¤nger als 3 Sekunden braucht, um zu wissen, ob der Use Case â€žgutâ€œ oder â€žschlechtâ€œ lÃ¤uft, ist das Dashboard-Design gescheitert.
  - internal\vision\ACTIONREADY_HOLISTIC_MANIFESTO.md:105: ### II. 30 Sekunden (Die diagnostische Logik â€“ System 2 â€žLightâ€œ)
  - internal\vision\ACTIONREADY_HOLISTIC_MANIFESTO.md:107: - **Ziel**: Identifikation des Treibers. â€žWo genau liegt das Problem?â€œ
